## 7. Questions for Mark at the gate

Numbered G-80 to G-150. The era 10 gate records `CiC_Step0_Era10_V2_0.md` and `CiC_Step0_Era10_Movements_V1_0.md` use G-1 to G-79. There is one question per candidate that has a gate question in its region's file, so the lettered options and the recommendation are the region's own. Africa's C11, C12 and C13, Latin America's LA-13, and the East's E11 and E12 have no gate question in their regions' files and get none here. The East's E3b shares E3a's question. The recommendation letter is the region's; where a region added a condition, it follows the letter. G-132 to G-135 are cross-region questions. G-132 (what IX.11 becomes after the sweep) and G-133 (where Catholic charismatic movements go) were added after Round 1, in the places of two questions that only restated G-105 and G-107. G-134 and G-135 are the issues the regions flagged; their recommendations are Round 1's. G-136 to G-150 are the supplementary pass's fifteen questions, one per candidate, in its own ranked order. That makes 52 regional candidate questions, 4 cross-cutting questions and 15 supplementary questions: 71 in all.

### Africa

- **G-80. Lane of the Catholic Church in Africa (C1)** Which lane does the Catholic Church in Africa sit in? (a) Lane 5, Latin West & Catholicism, as a regional chapter beside IX.47/IX.23, which shows the Catholic river reaching Africa; (b) Lane 4, Africa, beside IX.4/IX.10/IX.20, which keeps the Africa lane whole; (c) A 4<->5 bridge, as IX.21 uses 3<->5. **Rec. (c)** The gap Mark named is a river gap and a region gap at once. A bridge shows both and has precedent. The other Catholic regional rows (LA-1, GS-A4, GS-A5, GS-A6, GS-A8, GS-A14, E2, and SUP-1) all sit in lane 5, so this is one cross-cutting choice: a bridge for Africa alone, or the same lane for every Catholic regional row.
- **G-81. End of the apartheid window (C2)** Where does the window end? (a) 1994, the first democratic election, a clean real end like IX.6's 1945; (b) c. 1998, to include the TRC's faith-community hearings, when the churches confessed their own part; (c) c. 2010, as "South African Christianity after apartheid", which is a different row. **Rec. (a)** Put the TRC hearings in the legacy as a dated later event inside the era.
- **G-82. The African charismatic wave (C3)** How should the tree show the African charismatic wave? (a) Its own row, a bounded window of the kind IX.11's copy asks for; (b) A named line in IX.11, with Ojo and Asamoah-Gyadu added as its sources; (c) Hold until its own dated founding texts are found. **Rec. (a)** With (c)'s sourcing step done before entry. Four verified studies by African scholars recognize it as a movement distinct from both the Aladura and classical Pentecostalism, and a one-sentence line inside a global umbrella understates the continent where the century's Pentecostal growth is centered.
- **G-83. The churches and the 1994 genocide in Rwanda (C4)** How does the tree hold the churches and the 1994 genocide? (a) A named line in C1 and a relations line in IX.3; (b) Its own Tier 2 row, 1900-1994, with Longman's thesis tagged Contested; (c) Both: the row, plus cross-lines in C1 and IX.3. **Rec. (a)** If C1 enters, because it avoids a double count; (b) if C1 does not.
- **G-84. Africa's classical Pentecostal churches (C5)** Where do Africa's classical Pentecostal churches (AFM 1908; Church of Pentecost 1937/62) go? (a) An AFM row, plus the Church of Pentecost as an IX.11 line; (b) One "Classical Pentecostal churches in Africa" family row; (c) Lines in IX.11 only, with IX.11's start moved to 1908. **Rec. (a)** (b) is a trend-shaped family, and (c) changes a settled row's window.
- **G-85. Carrying VIII.42 into era 10 (C6)** How does the tree carry VIII.42 into era 10? (a) Enter "Malagasy Christianity and the Fifohazana" as VIII.42's era 10 successor; (b) Leave VIII.42 as a deliberate end at the 1895 French conquest; (c) Hold for a Malagasy own-voice source, then (a). **Rec. (c)** Then (a). The end at 1895 is principled for a kingdom-church, but the church itself went on and gave Africa one of its largest indigenous revivals.
- **G-86. Sudanese Christianity (C7)** Where does the Sudan story sit? (a) Its own row, 1955-2005; (b) A named line in IX.52 (Anglican) and C1 (Catholic); (c) Hold. **Rec. (a)** The conversion story crosses the churches, and the scholarship tells it as one Sudanese church's story.
- **G-87. Eastern Orthodoxy in sub-Saharan Africa (C8)** How is Eastern Orthodoxy in sub-Saharan Africa shown? (a) A named line in IX.4; (b) Its own Tier 3 row in lane 3; (c) Hold for Greek-language and Luganda sources. **Rec. (a)** Now, with (b) when sourced.
- **G-88. African theology: line or row (C9)** Is African theology a line or a row? (a) Lines in IX.46, IX.24 and C1; (b) Its own row, as a defined movement. **Rec. (a)** Consistent with the liberation-theology ruling.
- **G-89. The Maghreb church (C10)** What is done with the Maghreb church? (a) Hold, with Bazin to the wants list; (b) A named line in C1; (c) Enter as Tier 3 now. **Rec. (a)** Hold, with Bazin 1921 to the source-research wants list.

### Latin America and the Caribbean

- **G-90. Latin American Catholicism, 1906-2010 (LA-1)** How should the Latin American Catholic church of 1906-2010 enter? (a) One continental church row, 1906-c. 2010, receiving VIII.48, with IX.8 and the Cristero War as its children, and with Medellín and Puebla moving from IX.8 to this row as institutional acts while IX.8 keeps the theology and the communities (a change to IX.8); (b) Two rows split at 1955 (before and after CELAM); (c) Country rows (Mexico, Brazil) only; (d) No row, widen IX.23's regions and add lines. **Rec. (a)** It mirrors VIII.48's shape and closes the receiver gap V2.0 §1A named; (b) splits a story whose own documents (Rio 1955 to Aparecida 2007) read as one chain, and (d) leaves 1906-1962 empty.
- **G-91. Brazil's Pentecostal century (LA-2)** Where does Brazil's Pentecostal century go? (a) Its own row, 1910-c. 2010, with the IURD as its third-wave closing line; IX.11's documented Pará story ("Two Swedes Sail for Pará", on Alencar 2010) moves to this row by a named change order, and IX.11 keeps one pointer line (see G-132) (rule with G-132); (b) Named lines in IX.11 only, and IX.11's start moves to 1910; (c) Two rows: classical (CCB, AD, 1910-) and neo-Pentecostal (IURD, 1977-). **Rec. (a)** IX.11's own rule asks for a bounded window, one country. One row matches the Brazilian scholarship, which tells the waves as one story; (c) would rest on a typology that scholars dispute.
- **G-92. Chile's Pentecostal church (LA-3)** Where does Chile's Pentecostal church go? (a) Its own row, 1909-c. 2010, Tier 2; (b) A named line in IX.2 or IX.11; (c) One "Latin American classical Pentecostalism" row joining Chile and Brazil. **Rec. (a)** Once a dated statement settles Section A. The two national stories differ in origin and scholars study them apart; (c) would break IX.11's one-country rule.
- **G-93. The Cristero War (LA-4)** How does the Cristero War enter? (a) Its own row, 1926-1929, living false, closing on the 2000 canonization; (b) A named line inside LA-1; (c) Its own row, 1926-c. 1938, including the second rising. **Rec. (a)** Once one dated own text of the movement is staged (the Liga Nacional Defensora de la Libertad Religiosa's 1925 program or a dated *cristero* manifesto). It shows all four marks on verified items and has Meyer's standard study, and the roster already takes episode rows (the Confessing Church, 1933-45), but the evidence rule needs the movement's own dated founding text and that precedent does not waive it. Until the text is staged, (b) is the fallback; (c) rests on an [S] date.
- **G-94. Latin American Protestantism (LA-5)** How does Latin American Protestantism enter? (a) One family row, 1916-c. 2010, Tier 2; (b) Named lines in IX.24 (FTL, CLADE) and IX.46 (Panama 1916, CLAI); (c) Hold until a dated living item and an ISAL source are verified. **Rec. (a)** Its dated acts are verified and Bastian is the field's standard; (b) would scatter one region's story across two North-Atlantic rows.
- **G-95. Catholic Charismatic Renewal in Latin America (LA-6)** Where does the Catholic Charismatic Renewal in Latin America go? (a) A named line in IX.7, and Latin America added to its regions; (b) Its own row, 1969-c. 2010, on Chesnut; (c) A line in LA-1. **Rec. (a)** IX.7 already holds the CCR as one of its four movements; a separate row would split one movement by continent. GS-A6 places El Shaddai by a different rule; G-133 asks for one rule.
- **G-96. The Caribbean churches (LA-7)** Do the Caribbean churches enter now? (a) Enter now, 1906-c. 2010, Tier 2, on Dayfoot, the MCCA and the CCC; (b) Hold until a post-1962 study is verified (Davis 1990 or another), then enter; (c) Named lines in IX.52 and IX.46 only. **Rec. (b)** Dayfoot stops at 1962, and the half-century after independence rests on two institutional dates alone.
- **G-97. Afro-Caribbean churches (LA-8)** Do the Spiritual (Shouter) Baptists and Jamaican Revival enter now? (a) Hold: put a dated Spiritual Baptist statement of faith (or a study that sets one out) and Zane or Glazier on the wanted list, enter Tier 2 once A clears; (b) Enter now, with Section A settled by Mark under A5; (c) A named line inside LA-7. **Rec. (a)** The persecution story is dated and verified, but Section A cannot be judged on anything seen, and the floor is Mark's.
- **G-98. Mexican Oneness Pentecostalism (LA-9)** Where does the Iglesia Apostólica de la Fe en Cristo Jesús (1914) go? (a) A named line in IX.15, and Mexico added to its countries; (b) Its own register row; (c) No change. **Rec. (a)** IX.15 already points to it; the line would name what the pointer refers to.
- **G-99. Ethiopian Orthodox Church in the Caribbean (LA-10)** Where does the Ethiopian Orthodox Church in the Caribbean (1970) go? (a) A named line in IX.20, with Jamaica added to its countries; (b) A named line in LA-7; (c) No line. **Rec. (a)** It is the Ethiopian church's own Western Hemisphere mission.
- **G-100. Haiti, 1906-2010 (LA-11)** What is done with Haiti after VIII.46? (a) Hold, with Greene and a French-language source (for example Laënnec Hurbon's work [S]) on the wanted list; (b) A named line in LA-1 now, on Greene; (c) Nothing. **Rec. (a)** Hold.
- **G-101. Guatemalan evangelical growth (LA-12)** Where does Central American evangelical growth (Guatemala) go? (a) A line in LA-5; (b) A line in IX.11. **Rec. (a)** A line in LA-5.

### Asia and the Pacific

- **G-102. How the Korean Protestant century enters (GS-A1)** How should the Korean Protestant century enter? (a) One church-family row, 1907-c. 2010, carrying the March First Movement, the shrine crisis, the post-war Presbyterian divisions, minjung theology (as a named line under the scope rule) and the growth decades, Yoido stays in IX.11; (b) One "Korean Christianity in the twentieth century" row holding Protestants and Catholics together (folds GS-A5); (c) Named lines only, in IX.2 and IX.11. **Rec. (a)** The Protestant and Catholic stories have separate carriers, dates and texts; (c) repeats the silence the sweep exists to end.
- **G-103. South Asia's united churches (GS-A2)** How should South Asia's united churches enter? (a) One row for the CSI and CNI together (with the Church of Pakistan as a named line), 1919/1947-c. 2010, opening on the Dalit mass movements as its people (GS-A3 folded as a named line); (b) The CSI alone as a row, the CNI as a named line; (c) A named line inside IX.46 (the ecumenical movement). **Rec. (a)** The two unions are one negotiated tradition (1919-1970), and (c) would make the subject of the story a footnote to Geneva.
- **G-104. Dalit and Adivasi mass movements (GS-A3)** Where do the Dalit mass movements go? (a) A named line opening GS-A2; (b) Their own movement row, c. 1870s-c. 1940, spanning eras 9 and 10; (c) Hold. **Rec. (a)** Their decisive history is the people the united churches were made of. (b) is defensible on the four marks and is the better choice if GS-A2 is declined, but it is not yet eligible: the evidence rule also needs the movement's own dated texts, which are wanted.
- **G-105. China's Catholics (GS-A4)** How should China's Catholics enter? (a) Their own row, 1906-c. 2010; (b) Widen IX.5 into "Chinese Christianity" with a Catholic wing (and reconsider its Pentecostal lane); (c) A named line in IX.47. **Rec. (a)** IX.5 is built on Protestant founding texts; folding Catholics in would double its subject and leave the lane wrong for both.
- **G-106. Korean Catholicism after 1886 (GS-A5)** Where does Korean Catholicism after 1886 go? (a) Its own row, 1906-c. 2010, with a disclosure that 1886-1906 is unmapped; (b) Its own row from 1886, crossing the era boundary as some rows do; (c) A wing of GS-A1 (option b there). **Rec. (a)** Once one scholarly study is verified; (b) moves a frozen era-9 boundary question and is Mark's.
- **G-107. Philippine Catholicism and El Shaddai (GS-A6)** How should the Philippine Catholic century enter, and where does El Shaddai belong? (a) Own row, El Shaddai becomes a named line here, and IX.11 keeps a one-line cross-reference; (b) Own row, El Shaddai stays in IX.11 untouched; (c) Named lines in IX.23 and IX.7 only. **Rec. (a)** Wiegele's title calls it "popular Catholicism"; its home is the Catholic river. LA-6 places the Latin American CCR by a different rule; G-133 asks for one rule.
- **G-108. The Philippine Independent Church (GS-A7)** Does the IFI get its own era-10 row? (a) Own row, 1906-c. 2010, lane 5<->6; (b) A named line in GS-A6; (c) A named line in IX.52. **Rec. (a)** It is a separate church with its own confession; (b) would file it under the church it left.
- **G-109. Vietnam (GS-A8)** How should Vietnam enter? (a) One row, two wings (Catholic, Evangelical Church), Hmong Protestants as a named line; (b) A Catholic row only, Protestants held; (c) Hold both for Vietnamese-language sources. **Rec. (a)** Once the Evangelical wing's Section A is settled on a dated statement. Each wing has a verified university-press study.
- **G-110. Japan after 1906 (GS-A9)** How should Japan after 1906 enter? (a) One family row like VIII.45: the Kyodan as spine, Mukyōkai and the indigenous movements as named lines, Catholics and Orthodox as named lines; (b) The Kyodan alone as a row; (c) Keep the named gap open. **Rec. (a)** It mirrors VIII.45's shape, so the chain reads straight on; the Spirit of Jesus Church is assessed separately for the floor.
- **G-111. Kerala's Eastern Catholics (GS-A10)** Do Kerala's Eastern Catholics get a row? (a) Hold for one verified study, then a row, lane 1<->5; (b) A named line in IX.39 now; (c) Enter now on the survey alone. **Rec. (a)** (c) would draft on Thin; (b) files a Catholic church under its Orthodox rivals.
- **G-112. Pacific Island churches (GS-A11)** How should the island churches enter? (a) One row, 1925-c. 2010, with the Brotherhood and the PCC as named lines; (b) The Melanesian Brotherhood alone as a movement row; (c) Named lines in IX.26 and IX.52. **Rec. (a)** Once Section A is settled on the member churches' dated statements. IX.26 is a register row for movements that left the mission churches; putting the mainline churches inside it would reverse its meaning.
- **G-113. Indian Pentecostalism (GS-A12)** Where does Indian Pentecostalism go? (a) A named line in IX.11, with Bergunder added to its sources; (b) Its own row; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** IX.11's own rule is "one body, one country, one generation"; this is that kind of window.
- **G-114. The FABC (GS-A13)** Does the FABC become a line in IX.23? (a) Yes, a named line; (b) Its own row; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** It is a body of bishops, not a lived world.
- **G-115. Catholics of Indonesia and Timor-Leste (GS-A14)** Where do Indonesia's and Timor-Leste's Catholics go? (a) Named lines in IX.37; (b) A Timor-Leste Catholic row; (c) Hold. **Rec. (a)** IX.37's title is "Indonesian Christianity", and Aritonang & Steenbrink treat both confessions together.
- **G-116. Australia and Aotearoa New Zealand (GS-A15)** What happens to Australasia? (a) Hold the row, place named lines now; (b) Draft a row on Breward alone; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** Hold the row and place named lines now.
- **G-117. The Christian ashram movement (GS-A16)** Is the Christian ashram movement a line in GS-A2? (a) Yes; (b) Own row; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** A named line in GS-A2.
- **G-118. Minjung theology (GS-A17)** Is minjung theology a line in GS-A1? (a) Yes; (b) No. **Rec. (a)** By the 2026-07-16 scope rule.
- **G-119. Presbyterian Church in Taiwan statements (GS-A18)** Are the Taiwan Presbyterian statements of 1971, 1975 and 1977 held? (a) Hold; (b) A named line in IX.5. **Rec. (a)** IX.5 is a mainland story.
- **G-120. Myanmar's Baptist peoples (GS-A19)** Does Myanmar's Baptist story go to source research? (a) Yes; (b) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** Send it to the source-research thread.
- **G-121. The Japanese Orthodox Church after Nikolai (GS-A20)** Is the Japanese Orthodox Church a line in GS-A9? (a) Yes; (b) Own row. **Rec. (a)** A named line in GS-A9, cross-referenced from IX.48.

### Eastern Europe, the former Soviet world and the Middle East

- **G-122. The Russian church's institutional story (E1)** How should the Russian church's institutional story enter era 10? (a) A new church row, 1917-c. 2010, successor of VIII.31, with IX.22 and IX.9 as its named kin; (b) Extend IX.22 to c. 2010 and widen it to the whole church, which blurs a defined martyrdom row; (c) Keep D-P2's "named gap, no row". **Rec. (a)** It closes the V2.0 D-P2 gap on sources that are now verified, and keeps IX.22 a persecution row.
- **G-123. East-Central European Catholicism (E2)** How should East-Central European Catholicism enter? (a) A Polish church row, 1918-c. 2010, with Lithuania as a named line (E12); (b) One composite row, "The Catholic Church under communism in East-Central Europe" (Poland, Lithuania, Czechoslovakia, Hungary, Croatia), 1945-1989, which is wider but has four of five legs [S] today and cuts Poland's 1918-45 and 1989-2010; (c) A Polish row now, and a composite row for the rest held for sources. **Rec. (a)** The Polish sources are verified now; the rest of the bloc is not.
- **G-124. The Balkan and Greek Orthodox churches (E3a, E3b)** One Balkan row or two? (a) One family row for the Balkan and Greek churches together, as VIII.28 had; (b) Two rows: E3a for the churches under communism (Serbia, Romania, Bulgaria, Albania) and E3b for the Church of Greece and the Ecumenical Patriarchate; (c) Four national rows, which the verified base does not yet support. **Rec. (b)** After 1945 the two halves lived under different political orders, and the Greek half's story (Zoe, the calendar schism, the Phanar in Turkey, 1965) does not share the communist half's sources or questions.
- **G-125. Registered Soviet evangelicals (E4)** Where do the registered Soviet evangelicals go? (a) A new family row, successor of VIII.51, with Pentecostals and the Romanian Pentecostal union as named lines; (b) Widen IX.12 from the dissident wing to the whole evangelical family, which merges a persecution row with a denominational one, the double-count IX.22/E1 avoids; (c) A named line on VIII.51 only ("continued after 1906"), no row. **Rec. (a)** Successor of VIII.51, entered after the 1985 confession is read for Section A. Its tier is 2 on B1-B5. The Protestant river's regional imbalance (section 1: 20 entries, 16 of them North American) is a reason to weigh entry, not a Section B criterion, so it is put here for Mark rather than in the tier.
- **G-126. The Holy Land's Arab churches (E7)** How should the Holy Land's Arab churches enter? (a) A church-region row 1948-c. 2010 with Sabeel/Kairos as named lines and the dispute carried from both sides' texts; (b) A movement row for Palestinian liberation theology only, which fails mark 4 today and would tell the region through one theology; (c) Hold for sources (Raheb, a study of the movement). **Rec. (a)** With a contested_claim record built before entry.
- **G-127. The Orthodox Youth Movement (E5)** Should the Antiochian Orthodox Youth Movement enter now? (a) Enter now as a Tier 2 renewal-movement row on the verified marks 1-3 and the Ebeid line; (b) Hold until a scholarly study of the movement and its 1942 principles in Arabic are read; (c) Make it a named line inside a wider "Arab Christianity of Lebanon and Syria" row with E6. **Rec. (b)** The evidence rule asks for both; mark 4 is partial.
- **G-128. The Maronite Church (E6)** Should the Maronite row enter now? (a) Enter now as Tier 2 on Salibi and the dated synods; (b) Hold until the Patriarchal Synod's texts and one dedicated history are read; (c) Fold with E5 into one "Christians of Lebanon and Syria" row. **Rec. (b)** Hold until the Patriarchal Synod's texts and one dedicated history are read.
- **G-129. Suppressed Greek Catholic churches (E8)** Where does the Romanian Greek Catholic Church go, and the other suppressed Greek Catholic churches? (a) A new row for the Romanian church, the other two (Prešov, Mukachevo) as IX.21 lines; (b) Rename and widen IX.21 to "The Suppressed Greek Catholic Churches, 1946-1990" (a change to an existing row); (c) Named lines in IX.21 only. **Rec. (a)** A new row for the Romanian church, the other two as IX.21 lines.
- **G-130. Ukrainian Orthodox autocephaly (E9)** Ukrainian autocephaly: row or line? (a) Own row now; (b) Named line in E1 now, row held; (c) Leave it out. **Rec. (b)** The dates rest on V-s items, and the canonical dispute needs both sides' texts.
- **G-131. The Chaldean Church in modern Iraq (E10)** How is the Chaldean Church in modern Iraq placed? (a) A line in IX.19; (b) Own row held; (c) None. **Rec. (a)** A line in IX.19, which already includes the Assyro-Chaldean delegation of 1919; a full row is held for a scholarly history.

### Cross-cutting

- **G-132. What IX.11 becomes after the sweep (IX.11; LA-2, LA-3, C3, C5, GS-A6, GS-A12)** IX.11 (Global South Pentecostal) is a multi-country umbrella. Its census record tells the Assembleias de Deus founding as a Tier 1 documented story, "Two Swedes Sail for Pará" (November 1910 - June 1911), on the Igreja Batista de Belém records and Alencar 2010; it names the IURD (1977) as a voice, carries El Shaddai (1984), and its placeLabel is "Brazil · Nigeria and beyond". Six candidates draw on it: LA-2 (the Pará story moves out), GS-A6 (El Shaddai moves out), C3 (a Nigerian row beside its one RCCG sentence), LA-3 (Chile, which it does not name), C5 (a Church of Pentecost line moves in) and GS-A12 (an Indian line moves in). What is IX.11 once these are ruled? (a) IX.11 stays the multi-country umbrella and keeps its stories, and the new country rows cross-reference it; (b) IX.11 narrows to what no country row holds, and its Brazil story and El Shaddai move to LA-2 and GS-A6 by a named change order, with pointer lines left behind; (c) Country rows are held until IX.11's scope is ruled. **Rec. (b)** IX.11's own copy says only a bounded window, "one body, one country, one generation", describes anything real, so country rows are what it asks for; keeping the Pará story in both rows would tell one documented story twice. A named change order keeps the move to a settled row explicit. (c) would hold every country row on a question the gate can rule at once. This recommendation is the reviser's, added after Round 1; Mark's call. Rule with G-91: G-91(a) as written assumes (b) here. If (a) is chosen here, G-91(a) enters LA-2 with a cross-reference to IX.11's Pará story instead of moving it.
- **G-133. Where Catholic charismatic movements go (LA-6, GS-A6; IX.7, IX.11)** LA-6 puts the Latin American Catholic Charismatic Renewal into IX.7, the movement row, because a separate row would split one movement by continent. GS-A6 moves El Shaddai, also a Catholic charismatic movement, out of IX.11 into the Philippine Catholic row. Which rule holds? (a) By movement: every Catholic charismatic movement sits in IX.7, so El Shaddai becomes an IX.7 line with a cross-reference from GS-A6; (b) By national church: each sits in its country's Catholic row where one exists (El Shaddai in GS-A6; Brazil's CCR in LA-1), with IX.7 holding the movement's origin and a cross-reference; (c) A stated mixed rule: a national wing of the worldwide Catholic Charismatic Renewal goes in IX.7, while a movement with its own founder, founding and name (El Shaddai, Mike Velarde, 1984) goes in its country's Catholic row; neither goes in IX.11, the Pentecostal family. **Rec. (c)** It keeps both regions' recommendations (G-95 and G-107) and states the rule they share: the CCR in Brazil is one movement that came from Duquesne, while Wiegele treats El Shaddai as a Philippine popular Catholicism of its own. Either single rule, (a) or (b), is also defensible. This recommendation is the reviser's, added after Round 1; Mark's call.
- **G-134. Census region value for the Caribbean (LA-7, LA-8, LA-10, LA-11)** Which census region value do the Caribbean rows take? LA-7 says the census region is "likely 'Latin America' or 'North America'", calls it a mapping call, and notes that VIII.46 (Haiti) uses "North America". The census uses "Latin America" for VIII.48; only VIII.46 (frozen, era 9) uses "North America"; no "Caribbean" value exists among the ten in use, so adding one changes the region scheme for every era. SUP-3 (Cuba) takes the same value. (a) "Latin America"; (b) "North America", as VIII.46 uses; (c) A new "Caribbean" region value, the label the Latin America sweep used for LA-7 and LA-8. **Rec. (a)** "Latin America", matching the sweep's own region "Latin America and the Caribbean". VIII.46's "North America" is a frozen era 9 value, to be raised as a change order if Mark wants it aligned. Recommended by Round 1; Mark's call.
- **G-135. Doc-hygiene flags on IX.7, IX.11 and IX.15 (LA-2, LA-6, LA-9)** The Latin America sweep flagged and did not touch three existing rows: IX.11 is dated "1950s-c. 2010" while its copy dates the Assembleias de Deus "from 1911"; IX.7 carries the Catholic Charismatic Renewal but no Latin American region; IX.15 names the 1914 Mexican Apostolic revival but carries no Latin American region or country. How are they handled? (a) Settle each with the matching gate answer: IX.11 with G-91, IX.7 with G-95, IX.15 with G-98; (b) Leave the three rows unchanged; (c) Fix IX.11's date range now as a data error, independent of G-91. **Rec. (a)** With IX.11's date mismatch fixed under (c) whatever G-91 rules, since the row contradicts its own copy. Recommended by Round 1; Mark's call.

### Supplementary pass

- **G-136. Maya Christianity (SUP-1)** How should the Maya churches of Guatemala and Chiapas enter era 10? (a) One regional window row, c. 1948-c. 2010, Catholic spine (Catholic Action, the catechists, San Cristóbal under Samuel Ruiz, REMHI and Gerardi), with the Maya Protestant churches and the Chamula expulsions as a named line; lane 5; (b) Named lines only: the catechists and REMHI in LA-1, the Protestant side in LA-12/LA-5; (c) Hold until a Maya-language or Maya-authored own text is found. **Rec. (a)** Once a dated own text is staged and the contested_claim record is built. (b) would leave era 10 with no indigenous American subject, the silence the review named; (c) sets a bar no other row meets, since Kovic's study is built on Maya testimony and Falla's on fieldwork in K'iche' communities.
- **G-137. The Persian-speaking church of Iran (SUP-2)** How should Iran's Persian-speaking Protestants enter era 10? (a) One national church-family row, 1912-c. 2010: the Anglican diocese and Dehqani-Tafti, the Evangelical Church, the Assemblies of God and the 1994 killings, the house churches and the diaspora; lane 6; (b) A named line in IX.19, which already tags Iran; (c) Hold for Persian-language sources. **Rec. (a)** Once Section A is settled. (b) would file Persian-speaking converts from Islam inside a Syriac genocide-and-diaspora row they do not belong to; (c) is the fallback if no Persian-language statement is found.
- **G-138. The churches in revolutionary Cuba (SUP-3)** How should the Cuban churches after 1959 enter? (a) Their own row, 1959-c. 2010, Catholic and Protestant together (lane 5<->6), as a child of LA-1 if LA-1 enters; (b) A named line in LA-1 for the Catholic side and one in LA-5 for the Protestants; (c) Hold until the ENEC document and a Protestant statement are read. **Rec. (a)** Once the ENEC final document is opened and the Protestant wing's Section A is settled on one dated statement. Three verified studies, one of them Cuban, carry it, and the subject is one story told from two rivers: churches under communism in the Americas. (b) splits it, and LA-1 and LA-5 are themselves not yet entered. Rule with G-90 (LA-1): if G-90(a) enters LA-1, this row is its child, as the Cristero row would be; if not, it stands alone.
- **G-139. The Christians of Pakistan (SUP-4)** Where do Pakistan's Christians after 1947 go? (a) Their own church-region row, 1947-c. 2010, with the Zabur and the mass movements as its roots; G-103(a)'s Church of Pakistan line moves here; (b) Keep the Church of Pakistan as a named line in GS-A2 and add the Catholic and blasphemy-law story there; (c) Hold for Urdu and Punjabi own-voice sources. **Rec. (a)** Once the Church of Pakistan's Basis of Union is opened for Section A. GS-A2 is a South Indian union story; Pakistan's church lived a different life as a minority in an Islamic state, and Walbridge carries it. (b) would overload a row about church union with a story about persecution. Rule with G-103 (GS-A2): under (a) here, G-103(a)'s Church of Pakistan line moves to this row with a cross-reference.
- **G-140. The Nepali church (SUP-5)** What happens to the Nepali church? (a) Hold, and send the Nepal Church History Project archive (Yale RG 215) and Perry's thesis to source research; (b) Enter now as a Tier 3 row on Perry; (c) A named line in IX.11. **Rec. (a)** Perry's book is the church's own history, so it is own voice, not the independent study the rule needs. (c) would assume a Pentecostal identity the sources seen do not establish.
- **G-141. Arab evangelical churches and the MECC (SUP-6)** What happens to the Arab evangelical churches and the MECC? (a)** Sharkey ends in 1967 and covers Egypt only; (c) would file Protestants inside a Coptic Orthodox revival row and a Palestinian row.
- **G-142. Andean and Amazonian indigenous Christianity (SUP-7)** Where do the Andean and Amazonian indigenous churches go? - **G-143. Myanmar check against GS-A19 (SUP-8)** Does this evidence change GS-A19's hold? - **G-146. Sri Lanka (SUP-11)** What happens to Sri Lanka? (a)** Stirrat is one anthropological study of shrines; the church's own and the island's own scholarship are unseen.
- **G-147. Bangladesh (SUP-12)** Where does Bangladesh go? (a) Named lines in GS-A2 (the Church of Bangladesh; the Garo Christians); (b) Named lines in SUP-4 if the Pakistan row enters (the Church of Bangladesh left the Church of Pakistan in 1974); (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** The Church of Bangladesh is a united church of GS-A2's family; (b) would tie Bangladesh to the state it seceded from.
- **G-148. Central Asia (SUP-13)** Where does Central Asia go? (a) Named lines in E4 (evangelicals, Pentecostals, deported Mennonites) and E1 (Orthodox parishes; deported Catholics and Lutherans as a cross-reference); (b) A 'Christians of Central Asia' row, held for Russian- and Kazakh-language sources; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** Peyrouse and Pelkmans read Central Asian Christianity as the fragments of Soviet and missionary churches, which is what E1 and E4 carry.
- **G-149. Cambodia and Laos (SUP-14)** What happens to Cambodia and Laos? (a) Hold both; flag the 1950 Lao Hmong origin for GS-A8's Hmong line when a study confirms it; (b) Cambodia's Khmer Rouge story as a named line in GS-A13 (Asian Catholicism after the Council); (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** Ponchaud is one missionary's history and Laos has no study verified; (b) files a martyrdom under a bishops' federation.
- **G-150. Mongolia (SUP-15)** What happens to Mongolia? (a) Hold for one scholarly study and a Mongolian-language source; (b) A named line in IX.11 for the Protestant churches; (c) Leave out. **Rec. (a)** (b) assumes a Pentecostal identity the sources seen do not establish.

## 8. Consolidated wanted list

Everything each region named for the source-research thread, grouped by region. Each group has two parts: the items the region listed as wanted or held for sources, and the evidence items the region marked [S], V-s or wanted inside its candidates (shortened; the full item and its URL are in sections 3 to 6). Early texts the region itself described as public domain by date are marked **public-domain early text**. Only Africa's Bazin 1921 and Latin America's 1917 Panama congress reports carry that description; Hoover's Chilean history is not marked, because its date and rights are still open.
